**Capstone Build III: Testing, Error Handling & Polish**

Student Name: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Roll No: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Date: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Marks: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Instructor: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Signature: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

**▍ Objective**

Finalise the capstone build by adding comprehensive testing, error handling, and polish to make it production-quality.

**▍ Theory / Background**

A working application becomes a production-quality one through testing, error handling, and polish. This stage hardens the capstone — finding and fixing bugs, handling failures gracefully, and refining the experience — so it is reliable and professional rather than fragile and rough.

Testing means systematically checking that the application works across realistic scenarios: normal inputs, edge cases, and unusual usage. For an AI app, this includes testing the AI features with varied inputs to ensure consistent, correct behaviour. Thorough testing catches problems before users do.

Error handling makes the application robust. Real applications face empty inputs, failed API calls, missing data, and unexpected usage. Graceful handling — clear messages, fallbacks, and recovery instead of crashes — is what separates a trustworthy product from a brittle prototype. Every external call should be wrapped.

Polish refines the experience: a clean interface, helpful guidance, loading indicators for slow AI calls, and consistent design. These finishing touches signal quality and make the application pleasant to use. Together, testing, error handling, and polish complete the transformation into an industry-ready solution.

**▍ Tools & Requirements**

| Platform | Full app stack |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Concept** | Testing, robustness, polish |
| **Deliverable** | A production-quality capstone |
| **Skill** | Harden and refine the application |

**▍ Step-by-Step Procedure**

1. Test the app with normal, edge-case, and unusual inputs.

2. Test the AI features with varied inputs for consistency.

3. Add error handling around every external call.

4. Handle empty inputs and missing data gracefully.

5. Add loading indicators and clear messages.

6. Polish the interface for a clean, consistent look.

7. Confirm the app is reliable end to end.

**▍ Python Code (Build III)**

| \# Capstone Build III: Testing, Error Handling, Polish   \# \--- ERROR HANDLING: wrap every external call \--- def safe\_answer(question):     if not question or not question.strip():         return "Please enter a question."          \# empty input     try:         \# result \= answer\_question(question)        \# AI/API call         result \= "(grounded answer)"         if not result:             return "No answer found. Try rephrasing."  \# missing data         return result     except Exception as e:         return f"Something went wrong. Please try again. ({type(e).\_\_name\_\_})"   \# \--- TESTING: normal, edge, and unusual cases \--- test\_inputs \= \["What is chapter 1 about?",   \# normal                "",                            \# edge: empty                "asdfghjkl",                   \# edge: nonsense                "x" \* 5000\]                    \# edge: very long for t in test\_inputs:     print(f"IN\[{t\[:20\]}\] \-\> {safe\_answer(t)\[:40\]}")   \# \--- POLISH checklist \--- print(""" Polish:   \[ \] Clean, consistent interface   \[ \] Loading indicator for slow AI calls   \[ \] Helpful placeholder / guidance text   \[ \] Friendly error messages (no raw tracebacks) """) print("Testing \+ error handling \+ polish \= production-quality.") |
| :---- |

**▍ Expected Output**

A production-quality capstone hardened with systematic testing across normal and edge cases, error handling wrapped around every external call, graceful handling of empty or missing data, and interface polish — confirmed reliable end to end.

**▍ Practice Tasks**

1. Task 1: Add testing, error handling, and polish to your capstone so it handles edge cases gracefully and feels production-quality.

2. Task 2: Test your AI features with at least four varied inputs including edge cases, and fix any issues.

3. Task 3: List the error cases your app now handles and how it responds to each.

**▍ Viva Questions & Answers**

**Q1. What turns a working app into a production-quality one?**

**Answer:** Systematic testing, robust error handling, and polish that make it reliable and professional.

**Q2. What does thorough testing involve?**

**Answer:** Checking the app across normal inputs, edge cases, and unusual usage, including varied AI inputs.

**Q3. Why is error handling essential?**

**Answer:** Real apps face empty inputs, failed calls, and missing data; graceful handling prevents crashes and builds trust.

**Q4. What does polish add?**

**Answer:** A clean interface, helpful guidance, loading indicators, and consistent design that signal quality.

**Q5. Which calls should be wrapped in error handling?**

**Answer:** Every external call, especially AI and API calls that can fail or return unexpected results.

**Capstone Build IV: Deployment & Going Live**

Student Name: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Roll No: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Date: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Marks: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

**▍ Objective**

Deploy the finished capstone to the cloud so it is live and accessible to users via a public URL.

**▍ Theory / Background**

The capstone becomes a real product when it goes live. Deployment takes the finished, polished application and hosts it on the cloud, giving it a public URL that anyone can access. This is the culmination of the build — the moment the solution becomes available to actual users.

Deployment applies the packaging and cloud skills from Module 9: packaging the app with its dependencies, configuring secrets securely on the hosting platform, and launching. For a capstone, this means the full application — interface, AI logic, and data — running reliably in a hosted environment.

Going live surfaces real-world concerns. The deployed app must handle real users, keep secrets secure, respect platform resource limits, and remain stable. Testing the live deployment thoroughly — as a fresh user, across features — confirms the product works outside the development environment.

A successfully deployed capstone is a complete, demonstrable AI product accessible to the world. This achievement represents the full arc of Level 2: from a real problem, through technical construction, to a live solution. It is the centrepiece of a portfolio and proof of end-to-end applied-AI capability.

**▍ Tools & Requirements**

| Platform | Streamlit Cloud / HF Spaces / Docker |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Concept** | Production deployment |
| **Deliverable** | A live capstone at a public URL |
| **Skill** | Take the solution live |

**▍ Step-by-Step Procedure**

1. Package the finished app with its requirements.

2. Push it to a code repository.

3. Connect the repository to a hosting platform.

4. Configure all secrets securely on the platform.

5. Launch the deployment and wait for the build.

6. Test the live app thoroughly as a fresh user.

7. Confirm all features work at the public URL.

**▍ Deployment (Go Live)**

| \# Capstone Build IV: Deployment & Going Live   \# \--- 1\. Package: ensure the repo has app.py \+ requirements.txt \--- """ requirements.txt:   streamlit==1.38.0   openai==1.40.0   pypdf==4.0.0   ...all pinned dependencies """   \# \--- 2\. Push to a repository \--- \#   git add . && git commit \-m "capstone deploy" && git push   \# \--- 3-4. Connect to platform \+ configure secrets \--- \#   Streamlit Cloud: New app \-\> repo \-\> Settings \-\> Secrets: \#       AI\_API\_KEY \= "..." \#   (or Docker: docker build \+ run on a server)   \# \--- 5\. Launch \-\> platform builds \+ serves the app \--- \#   \-\> Public URL: https://your-capstone.streamlit.app   \# \--- 6\. TEST the LIVE deployment \--- LIVE\_TEST\_CHECKLIST \= """ \[ \] App loads at the public URL \[ \] Each feature works through the live interface \[ \] Secrets work (AI calls succeed) but are not exposed \[ \] Handles a fresh user with no prior state \[ \] Stable across a few concurrent uses """ print(LIVE\_TEST\_CHECKLIST) print("Deployed capstone \= a live, accessible, complete AI product.") |
| :---- |

**▍ Expected Output**

A live capstone deployed to the cloud with a working public URL, secrets configured securely, and all features verified to work in the hosted environment when tested as a fresh user.

**▍ Practice Tasks**

2. Task 2: Test every feature of the live app as a fresh user and fix any deployment-specific issues.

**▍ Viva Questions & Answers**

**Q1. What does deploying the capstone achieve?**

**Answer:** It hosts the finished app on the cloud with a public URL, making the complete solution available to real users.

**Q2. What skills does deployment apply?**

**Answer:** Packaging with dependencies, configuring secrets securely on the platform, and launching from Module 9\.

**Q3. What real-world concerns does going live surface?**

**Answer:** Handling real users, keeping secrets secure, respecting resource limits, and remaining stable.

**Q4. Why test the live deployment as a fresh user?**

**Answer:** To confirm the product works outside the development environment, with no prior state or hidden setup.

**Q5. What does a deployed capstone represent?**

**Answer:** The full arc of Level 2 — from a real problem to a live, complete AI product and portfolio centrepiece.

**Capstone Documentation & Technical Write-Up**

Student Name: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Roll No: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Date: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Marks: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Instructor: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Signature: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

**▍ Objective**

Document the capstone with a technical write-up covering the problem, architecture, implementation, and results.

**▍ Theory / Background**

A professional project is documented, not just built. Documentation explains what the capstone does, how it works, and why it was built that way — making the work understandable to others and demonstrating engineering maturity. For a portfolio, strong documentation is as important as the product itself.

A technical write-up has a clear structure: the problem and its importance, the solution overview, the technical architecture, the implementation details (key components and decisions), the results, and the limitations and future work. This narrative lets a reader understand the whole project quickly.

Good documentation explains decisions, not just describes code. Why was RAG chosen over fine-tuning? Why this architecture? What trade-offs were made? Explaining the reasoning shows engineering judgement and is far more valuable than a mere feature list. It also helps others build on or maintain the work.

Honest reflection strengthens the write-up. Documenting limitations, challenges faced, and what you would improve demonstrates self-awareness and maturity. A write-up that includes both achievements and honest assessment is more credible and professional than one that only lists successes.

**▍ Tools & Requirements**

| Platform | Document editor / README |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Concept** | Technical documentation |
| **Deliverable** | A capstone technical write-up |
| **Skill** | Explain the what, how, and why |

**▍ Step-by-Step Procedure**

1. State the problem and why it matters.

2. Give a clear overview of the solution.

3. Document the technical architecture.

4. Explain key implementation components and decisions.

5. Present the results against the success criteria.

6. Note limitations and future improvements.

7. Assemble the write-up (and a README).

**▍ Write-Up Structure**

| \# Capstone Technical Write-Up (structure)   WRITE\_UP \= """ \# \[Capstone Title\]   \#\# 1\. Problem What problem, who it affects, and why it matters.   \#\# 2\. Solution Overview What the product does, in plain language.   \#\# 3\. Technical Architecture Components \+ how they connect (diagram):   Interface \-\> Core AI logic (RAG/agent) \-\> Data layer   \-\> LLM API \-\> Database \-\> Deployment   \#\# 4\. Implementation Key components and the DECISIONS behind them:   \- Why RAG (vs fine-tuning)?   \- Why this vector store / model / framework?   \- Key trade-offs made.   \#\# 5\. Results Performance against the success criteria. What works well; example outputs.   \#\# 6\. Limitations & Future Work Honest assessment of what is incomplete, challenges faced, and what you would improve next. """ print(WRITE\_UP) print("Document the WHAT, HOW, and especially the WHY.") |
| :---- |

**▍ Expected Output**

A capstone technical write-up covering the problem, solution overview, architecture, implementation with explained decisions, results against success criteria, and an honest assessment of limitations and future work.

**▍ Practice Tasks**

1. Task 1: Write a technical write-up for your capstone covering the problem, architecture, implementation decisions, results, and limitations.

2. Task 2: Explain one key technical decision in your capstone and the reasoning and trade-offs behind it.

3. Task 3: Document two honest limitations of your capstone and how you would address them.

**▍ Viva Questions & Answers**

**Q1. Why document a capstone project?**

**Answer:** To make the work understandable to others and demonstrate engineering maturity; for a portfolio it is as important as the product.

**Q2. What does a technical write-up include?**

**Answer:** The problem, solution overview, architecture, implementation details and decisions, results, and limitations and future work.

**Q3. Why explain decisions, not just describe code?**

**Answer:** Because explaining the reasoning and trade-offs shows engineering judgement, more valuable than a feature list.

**Q4. Why document limitations honestly?**

**Answer:** It demonstrates self-awareness and maturity, making the write-up more credible and professional.

**Q5. What three things should documentation explain?**

**Answer:** The what (the product), the how (the architecture and implementation), and especially the why (the decisions).

**LAB 70**  
**Week 24  |  Module 10: Capstone Project — Industry-Ready AI Solution**

**Capstone Presentation & Demo Day**

Student Name: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Roll No: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Date: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Marks: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Instructor: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Signature: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

**▍ Objective**

Present and demonstrate the completed capstone, communicating the problem, solution, and results to an audience.

**▍ Theory / Background**

Building a solution is only half the achievement; communicating it is the other half. The capstone presentation demonstrates the finished product and tells its story — the problem, the solution, how it was built, and the results. Strong presentation skills are essential for any technical professional.

An effective technical presentation has a clear arc: hook the audience with the problem, present the solution, show it working through a live demo, explain the interesting technical aspects, and close with results and learnings. The live demo is the centrepiece — showing rather than just telling.

Demonstrating a live product is powerful but requires preparation. A smooth demo needs the deployed app ready, a planned sequence of features to show, sample inputs prepared, and a fallback if something fails. Practising the demo ensures it showcases the product's value confidently.

Presenting also means engaging the audience: explaining clearly, handling questions, and conveying both what was built and what was learned. This capstone presentation is the culmination of Level 2 — a demonstration of a complete, working AI solution and the applied-AI mastery behind it.

**▍ Tools & Requirements**

| Platform | Slides \+ live deployed app |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Concept** | Technical presentation & demo |
| **Deliverable** | A capstone presentation \+ live demo |
| **Skill** | Communicate and demonstrate the work |

**▍ Step-by-Step Procedure**

1. Structure the presentation: problem, solution, demo, tech, results.

2. Open with the problem to hook the audience.

3. Present the solution clearly.

4. Prepare and rehearse a live demo of key features.

5. Explain the interesting technical aspects.

6. Close with results and key learnings.

7. Deliver the presentation and handle questions.

**▍ Presentation & Demo Plan**

| \# Capstone Presentation Structure   PRESENTATION \= """ Slide 1: Title \+ one-line description Slide 2: THE PROBLEM (hook the audience — why it matters) Slide 3: THE SOLUTION (what you built, in plain language) Slide 4: LIVE DEMO  \<-- the centrepiece (show it working\!) Slide 5: HOW IT WORKS (architecture \+ interesting tech) Slide 6: RESULTS (against success criteria) Slide 7: LEARNINGS \+ future work """ print(PRESENTATION)   DEMO\_PREP \= """ Live demo readiness:   \[ \] Deployed app open and working   \[ \] Sequence of features planned   \[ \] Sample inputs prepared in advance   \[ \] Fallback ready if a live call fails   \[ \] Rehearsed end to end """ print(DEMO\_PREP) print("Show, don't just tell: the live demo proves the product works.") |
| :---- |

**▍ Expected Output**

A capstone presentation following a clear arc (problem, solution, live demo, technical explanation, results, learnings) with a rehearsed live demonstration of the deployed product and prepared handling of questions.

**▍ Practice Tasks**

1. Task 1: Deliver a capstone presentation with a live demo covering the problem, solution, technical approach, and results.

2. Task 2: Prepare your live demo with a feature sequence, sample inputs, and a fallback, then rehearse it.

3. Task 3: Anticipate two questions an audience might ask and prepare clear answers.

**▍ Viva Questions & Answers**

**Q1. Why is presenting the capstone important?**

**Answer:** Communicating the work is half the achievement; strong presentation skills are essential for any technical professional.

**Q2. What is the arc of an effective technical presentation?**

**Answer:** Hook with the problem, present the solution, demo it live, explain the technical aspects, and close with results and learnings.

**Q3. Why is the live demo the centrepiece?**

**Answer:** Because showing the product working is far more convincing than just describing it.

**Q4. What does a smooth demo require?**

**Answer:** The deployed app ready, a planned feature sequence, prepared inputs, a fallback, and rehearsal.

**Q5. What does the capstone presentation culminate?**

**Answer:** Level 2 — demonstrating a complete, working AI solution and the applied-AI mastery behind it.

**LAB 71**  
**Week 24  |  Module 10: Capstone Project — Industry-Ready AI Solution**

**Building Your AI Portfolio & Professional Profile**

Student Name: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Roll No: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Date: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Marks: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Instructor: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Signature: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

**▍ Objective**

Assemble an AI portfolio and professional profile that showcases the Level 2 projects and skills for career opportunities.

**▍ Theory / Background**

Skills become opportunities when they are visible. An AI portfolio showcases your projects and capabilities to potential employers, clients, or collaborators, turning what you have learned into career value. The Level 2 projects — RAG systems, agents, deployed apps, and the capstone — are strong portfolio material.

A good portfolio presents projects effectively: each with a clear description of the problem solved, the technologies used, your role, and a link to a live demo or code. Quality matters more than quantity — a few well-presented, working projects outweigh many half-finished ones. The capstone is the showcase piece.

A professional profile complements the portfolio. This includes a concise summary of your AI skills, your project highlights, and presence on relevant platforms (a code repository profile, a professional network). Clear, honest self-presentation of what you can build makes you discoverable and credible.

Communicating skills effectively is itself a skill. Describing projects in terms of the value delivered and the technologies mastered — not just features — resonates with employers. Assembling this portfolio and profile is the practical bridge from learning applied AI to using it in a career or venture.

**▍ Tools & Requirements**

| Platform | Portfolio site / repo / profile |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Concept** | Portfolio & professional profile |
| **Deliverable** | An AI portfolio with projects |
| **Skill** | Showcase skills for opportunities |

**▍ Step-by-Step Procedure**

1. Gather your strongest Level 2 projects.

2. For each, write the problem, tech used, and your role.

3. Add links to live demos or code repositories.

4. Feature the capstone as the showcase piece.

5. Write a concise AI skills summary.

6. Set up a professional profile linking the portfolio.

7. Review for clear, honest, value-focused presentation.

**▍ Portfolio & Profile Template**

| \# AI Portfolio & Professional Profile   \# \--- Portfolio project entry template \--- def portfolio\_entry(name, problem, tech, role, link):     return f""" \#\#\# {name} \- Problem : {problem} \- Tech    : {tech} \- My role : {role} \- Demo/Code: {link} """   print(portfolio\_entry(     "AI Study Assistant (Capstone)",     "Students struggle to study long course PDFs",     "Python, RAG, embeddings, Streamlit, deployed on cloud",     "Designed and built the full solution end to end",     "https://your-capstone.streamlit.app"))   \# \--- Skills summary (value \+ technologies) \--- SUMMARY \= """ Applied AI developer. I build and deploy AI solutions: \- RAG systems over documents (embeddings, vector search) \- AI agents with tools, planning, and safety guardrails \- AI-powered apps with APIs, integrated and deployed live """ print(SUMMARY)   \# \--- Profile presence \--- print("Profile: code repo (GitHub) \+ professional network \+ portfolio link") print("Present projects by VALUE delivered \+ tech mastered.") |
| :---- |

**▍ Expected Output**

An AI portfolio featuring the strongest Level 2 projects — each with problem, technologies, role, and a live demo or code link — headlined by the capstone, plus a concise skills summary and a professional profile.

**▍ Practice Tasks**

1. Task 1: Assemble an AI portfolio with at least two Level 2 projects, each with a clear description and a demo or code link, headlined by your capstone.

2. Task 2: Write a concise AI skills summary that describes value delivered and technologies mastered.

3. Task 3: Set up or update a professional profile that links to your portfolio.

**▍ Viva Questions & Answers**

**Q1. Why build an AI portfolio?**

**Answer:** To make skills visible and turn what you have learned into career value for employers, clients, or collaborators.

**Q2. How should each portfolio project be presented?**

**Answer:** With the problem solved, technologies used, your role, and a link to a live demo or code.

**Q3. Why does quality matter more than quantity?**

**Answer:** A few well-presented, working projects outweigh many half-finished ones in demonstrating capability.

**Q4. What complements the portfolio?**

**Answer:** A professional profile with a skills summary and presence on relevant platforms like a code repository.

**Q5. How should projects be described to employers?**

**Answer:** In terms of the value delivered and technologies mastered, not just a list of features.

**LAB 72**  
**Week 24  |  Module 10: Capstone Project — Industry-Ready AI Solution**

**Course Completion, Reflection & AI Career Pathways**

Student Name: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Roll No: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Date: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Marks: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Instructor: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_Signature: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

**▍ Objective**

Reflect on the complete Level 2 journey, consolidate the applied-AI skill set, and map next steps and career pathways in AI.

**▍ Theory / Background**

Level 2 concludes with reflection and forward planning. This final session consolidates the entire applied-AI journey — from ML foundations and responsible AI, through prompt engineering, data and APIs, automation, RAG, agents, app development, and deployment — and looks ahead to where these skills can lead.

Consolidation means recognising how far the skills have come. A Level 2 graduate can build and deploy real AI applications: RAG systems over documents, autonomous agents, integrated AI-powered apps, and a complete industry-ready capstone. Seeing this full skill set reinforces genuine, demonstrable capability.

Career pathways in applied AI are broad and growing. Roles include AI application developer, prompt engineer, AI automation specialist, RAG/LLM engineer, and AI product builder. The skills also enable entrepreneurship — building AI products and services. Mapping these paths turns capability into direction.

Continued growth is the final theme. AI evolves rapidly, so the most valuable habit is ongoing learning — following developments, building projects, and deepening skills. With a strong applied-AI foundation, a portfolio, and a plan for continued learning, the Level 2 graduate is equipped to pursue real opportunities in AI.

**▍ Tools & Requirements**

| Platform | Reflection document / plan |
| :---- | :---- |
| **Concept** | Consolidation & career mapping |
| **Deliverable** | A reflection \+ AI career plan |
| **Skill** | Map skills to next steps |

**▍ Step-by-Step Procedure**

1. Review the full Level 2 journey and skills gained.

2. List the kinds of AI applications you can now build.

3. Identify your strongest areas and interests.

4. Map relevant AI career pathways.

5. Choose a direction that fits your interests.

6. Plan continued learning and next projects.

7. Write a reflection and forward plan.

**▍ Reflection & Career Plan**

| \# Course Completion: Reflection & Career Pathways   \# \--- What a Level 2 graduate can now build \--- CAPABILITIES \= \[     "RAG systems over documents (embeddings \+ vector search)",     "Autonomous AI agents with tools, planning, and guardrails",     "AI-powered apps integrated with APIs and data",     "Cloud-deployed, production-quality AI solutions",     "A complete industry-ready capstone project", \] print("I can now build:") for c in CAPABILITIES: print("  \-", c)   \# \--- AI career pathways \--- PATHWAYS \= {     "AI Application Developer": "build AI-powered products",     "Prompt Engineer":          "design reliable AI prompting",     "AI Automation Specialist": "automate workflows with AI",     "RAG / LLM Engineer":       "build knowledge \+ agent systems",     "AI Product Builder / Founder": "create AI products & ventures", } print("\\nCareer pathways:") for role, focus in PATHWAYS.items():     print(f"  {role:\<26} \-\> {focus}")   \# \--- Continued-growth plan \--- PLAN \= """ Next steps:   \[ \] Chosen direction: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_   \[ \] Next project to build: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_   \[ \] Skills to deepen: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_   \[ \] Keep learning: follow AI developments \+ build regularly """ print(PLAN) print("Strong foundation \+ portfolio \+ learning plan \= ready for AI careers.") |
| :---- |

**▍ Expected Output**

A reflection consolidating the Level 2 journey and the AI applications the graduate can now build, a mapping of relevant AI career pathways, a chosen direction, and a concrete plan for continued learning and next projects.

**▍ Practice Tasks**

1. Task 1: Write a reflection on your Level 2 journey listing what you can now build, then map AI career pathways and choose a direction with a continued-learning plan.

2. Task 2: Identify your single strongest applied-AI skill and a role where it is most valuable.

3. Task 3: Plan your next AI project and the new skill it would help you develop.

**▍ Viva Questions & Answers**

**Q1. What does Level 2 consolidate?**

**Answer:** The full applied-AI journey — ML foundations, responsible AI, prompting, data and APIs, automation, RAG, agents, apps, and deployment.

**Q2. What can a Level 2 graduate build?**

**Answer:** RAG systems, autonomous agents, integrated AI-powered apps, and a complete deployed industry-ready capstone.

**Q3. Name three AI career pathways.**

**Answer:** AI application developer, prompt engineer, and RAG/LLM engineer; AI automation specialist and product builder are others.

**Q4. How do these skills enable entrepreneurship?**

**Answer:** They allow building AI products and services, opening a path to creating ventures, not just employment.

**Q5. What is the most valuable habit going forward?**

**Answer:** Ongoing learning — following AI developments, building projects, and deepening skills as the field evolves.
